**Watch items for the release-draft phone session (not fixes yet; owner, 18 Oct 2026):**
- **Stop → transcript:** measured median 4.2 s / p95 5.2 s on localhost against the 1.5 s budget (`05` §10). `netlify dev` adds about 1 s per `/voice` call in the owner's logs, so the figure is inflated. **Measure it on the phone; if it is still over budget there, it becomes a ticket.**
- **App cold start:** p95 27 s was dev-server first loads. The phone session gives the real figure (budget < 2 s).
- **Baseline note:** `?try=` dev bills count as hand-only "typing" bills in the baseline - harmless for a fresh pilot shop (recorded in `05` §10 and D80).

### 3. What remains before a production deploy

0. **Owner, now:** run `npx supabase db push --dry-run`, then `db push` (two additive migrations: `sync_probe` and `shops_one_per_owner` - first run the duplicate-owner check in `17` "Release day"); tell the agent the **Netlify credit balance** (credits reset 18 Oct) so the release draft can be built; and look at the **Google consent screen** (`17` section B): self sign-up needs it published, and Google's pages say a production app needs a homepage and a privacy-policy link - **an open item** (`KB-347` is parked).
1. **Netlify credits (owner, dashboard).** A production deploy is 15 credits; at 0 the Free plan pauses the site. Keep a second 15 for a worker emergency. (The 10 Oct note said "4 left until 18 Oct" — confirm the balance after the reset.)
2. **`KB-318` and `KI-50` (Gemini)** — waiting for the owner's go-ahead; nothing is built or run on it until he says so. Layer 2 parses fail on the free tier, so `KB-318` cannot be verified before it, and `KI-52` (a different product picked with no flag) is a wrong-number risk the thesis forbids.
3. **The release draft.** One `netlify deploy` (no `--prod`) from the release-candidate commit, built with the production `VITE_*` values (names and lengths only), then the **full phone session on that one build**: install, Google sign-in returning into the app, offline open, an offline bill syncing, reload mid-bill, sign-out count, Clear bill, a wiped phone getting its bills and learning back, Settings edits, Catalog edit and import with the real Android file picker, a live voice order through Layer 1 and Layer 2, receipt Image / PDF / WhatsApp, "Storage: protected", `?nosw=1`, the update flow including the foreground-check fix (two builds; the first with the phone offline at a foreground), and the four unmeasured 05 §10 budgets with `KB-404`'s timings. Add: the three screen languages on the phone (Devanagari wrapping), and the `KB-335` checks above.
4. **The deploy.** The owner signs off the exact commit; **one** commit carrying the deploy marker is pushed (15 credits). Note the previous production deploy's ID first (the rollback is "publish" that deploy). Then read the Netlify build log (`NI-29`), `curl -I` for `/sw.js` (`no-cache`) and `/manifest.webmanifest` (`application/manifest+json`), `/voice` answers 401, the app opens on the phone and Settings shows the new version. A rollback to the pre-worker deploy makes `/sw.js` a 404 — not tested, do not rely on it.
5. **Re-run on the release commit:** the official 3-worker `npm test`, `test:e2e`, `test:rls`, build, audit, and `eval:real` / `bench:numbers` / `coverage:probe` not worse than the baseline.
6. **After the release draft, not before:** the Whisper prompt before/after (`eval:audio`); it stays OFF until measured.

### 4. The owner's pre-pilot items

- **Privacy notice — DPDP Act 2023** (customer names and mobiles are personal data; the shop is the fiduciary): the notice a shop gives customers, retention vs immutable bills, processors, and WhatsApp/PDF sharing. Qualified advice, not the agent's.
- **Onboard the pilot shop** with the Ready catalog (a **fresh** shop; the live test shop is start-empty), after the `KI-53` owner-led catalog review. Sign-up policy: keep sign-ups off and create each pilot user yourself (proven by the `KB-335` draft test).
- **Redirect-URL cleanup, in this order, AFTER the last draft:** remove `https://*--kiranabilling.netlify.app/**` and `https://kiranabilling.netlify.app/**`, leaving the exact `https://kiranabilling.netlify.app/` (Site URL `https://kiranabilling.netlify.app`); sign in once more on production. (`http://localhost:5173/**` is already removed.)
- **Gemini billing / `KB-318` (`KI-50`)** — the owner is weighing alternatives and decides when everything else is done. **No Gemini work until he says so.**
- Per pilot phone: install and confirm "Storage: protected" (`KI-68`). Turn on the weekly backup look (`M-38`; consider healthchecks.io if the laptop will be off for days). GST (`NI-35`) only if a pilot shop is GST-registered; iPhone (`NI-30`) only if a pilot shop uses one.
- After the pilot starts: `KB-320`, `KB-322`, `KB-333`.

## Production release 28 Oct 2026 - rollback target and redirect URLs (owner approved: code `37e48cc`, deploy from HEAD)

- **Rollback target (recorded BEFORE the `[deploy]` marker):** the production deploy that is live today is **`6ac0b4f59e01a9000802865b`** (context production, state ready, built from `6e29cf2` "KI-61", published 3 Oct 2026; site `kiranabilling`, id `73e3f7d0-7f12-434e-813e-36a1dcc0d982`). Read from the Netlify API on 28 Oct 2026.
- **Rollback steps (no build, no credits):** Netlify -> Projects -> `kiranabilling` -> **Deploys** -> open deploy `6ac0b4f59e01a9000802865b` (3 Oct, `6e29cf2`) -> **Publish deploy**. That republishes its files AND its functions (the old `/voice`). Then check `/` and Settings show the old build. Do not roll back by reverting git: a revert commit would need another `[deploy]` marker and credits.
- **What a rollback leaves behind:** nothing in the database is undone. The migrations pushed since (D55 section 5: all additive) stay; the old app ignores the new columns and tables. Phones that already updated to the new service worker take the old one on their next launch like any update. A bill number the new app issued stays a valid number.
- **Released 28 Oct 2026:** PRODUCTION = `37e48cc` via `e6b230d` (the `[deploy]` commit), 28 Oct 2026, deploy `6ae1e5de507e259d58c6e891`. Netlify build: Node 24.22.1 / npm 11.19.1, the ignore script built on the marker, `tsc` + `vite build` OK, service worker 28 files precached, `voice.mts` bundled, secrets scan clean, "Site is live", ~45-48 s, **~15 credits**. Post-deploy checks (Claude, 28 Oct): `/`, `/privacy`, `/sw.js`, `/manifest.webmanifest` 200; `GET /voice` 405; `POST /voice` with no token 401 and with a forged token 401; the served bundle `index-BuPfkwbJ.js` (861,607 bytes) carries the build id `20261028-1209-e6b230d`, the project URL once, no local URLs, and the KB-353 "checking the token" row; `e6b230d` differs from `37e48cc` only in `docs/10-TRACKER.md` (no code file). Owner's release steps done: the live app on his phone on the new version (one voice bill, one hand bill); **Google app PUBLISHED; Supabase sign-ups ON; a never-used Gmail signed up on production and made Bill 1.** Still open: the redirect-URL cleanup (below, after the last draft).
- **Release 2 (29 Oct 2026) - rollback target, recorded BEFORE the marker:** the production deploy live until now is **`6ae1e5de507e259d58c6e891`** (`37e48cc` via `e6b230d`, published 28 Oct 2026). Rollback = the same Netlify steps as above (Deploys -> that deploy -> Publish deploy; no build, no credits). This release (`fb6d2e1`: the `KB-358-0` hotfix + `KB-358` a/b/c) changes no migration, so a rollback leaves the database as it is. The ever-older fallback is still `6ac0b4f59e01a9000802865b` (`6e29cf2`).
- **Redirect URLs (Supabase -> Authentication -> URL configuration), decision 28 Oct:** change NOTHING now. `KB-355` (voice speed) will need drafts again, so keep `https://*--kiranabilling.netlify.app/**` (the safe single-`*` form, D58 section 6). **After the LAST draft** the owner removes it AND `https://kiranabilling.netlify.app/**`, leaving the exact `https://kiranabilling.netlify.app/` (D82).

(one bare `127.0.0.1` — supabase-js's trusted-host list, expected), no dev string, and only the public **anon** key.

(`npx supabase migration list --linked`: all 18 match). **The draft uses the REAL cloud project and the live test shop** —
a new draft URL is a new origin, so the phone starts with an empty IndexedDB, exactly like a cleared phone.

**These steps create REAL live rows** (marked ⚠): final bills are immutable and cannot be deleted; products added to the
catalog cannot be removed in the app; edits to the shop row and a product price should be put back by hand.

Phone (Android, Chrome) and desktop, in this order. Tick each in the tracker when done.

   server…" (never "No bills on this phone yet."), fill newest first, and the hint should go. **Note the number of bills
   and roughly how many seconds until the hint went** (the laptop did ~0.8 s per 200 bills; the phone is unmeasured, D63 §6).
   Then: search a customer name, a receipt number and an amount; open a bill (receipt + four buttons); Android back should
   close the bill, then History, then return to billing.

   request → Response Headers → `content-encoding` (gzip / br, or none). **If none**, tell me: it goes into D63 §6 with the
   figures 36,000 bills ≈ 96 MB uncompressed vs ≈ 10 MB gzip.
4. [x] **Settings (KB-312) — phone.** ≡ → Settings. Try the phone `0712-2345678` (refused); choose हिन्दी, then open a past
   bill from History — the receipt is Hindi and keeps the new shop name. ⚠ Change the shop name to add " (test)", Save → "Saved".
   Airplane mode ON, change the name again, Save → "Saved on this phone — it will sync when you're online"; airplane mode OFF —

5. [x] **Catalog (KB-311) — phone.** ≡ → Catalog: search, the filters, a price edit (valid; `12.345` and `0` refused);
   airplane mode shows "Needs internet to change the catalog". ⚠ A price edit changes a real product — set it back.

## Release draft re-test — TWO phones, SHORT (owner) — after `KB-350` and `KB-351`

**Before you start (once):** `npx supabase db push` (dry run = exactly `20261022090000_bill_numbers`), `migration list --linked` shows it on both sides; Claude rebuilds the draft (same address, never `--prod`). On BOTH phones: open **Settings** and check the **new version** shows (close and reopen the app if not). Turn the Pilot numbers panel on (Developer mode) on A.

1. **Numbers in sequence.** A: Bill Banao -> note the number N. B: Bill Banao -> N+1. A again -> N+2. Check History on both phones: the same numbers, nothing doubled.
2. **A simultaneous Bill Banao.** Put a bill on screen on A and on B, count "3, 2, 1" and tap Bill Banao on both at the same moment. The two numbers must be consecutive (e.g. 5 and 6), never the same, never a jump.
3. **Offline keeps its number.** On A turn on airplane mode, make a bill: it saves AT ONCE with a code like `7F2A91-1` (never "pending"). Turn the network back on: the bill syncs and keeps that code; the next online bill continues the sequence.
4. **The step-8 price case.** On A change Chini's price (Catalog). Within ~30 s on B: a NEW Chini line (Add item, and by voice) uses the new price; a Chini line already on B's bill keeps the old one.
5. **An open Catalog on B.** Open Catalog on B and leave it. Change a price on A: B's list updates by itself. Then on B tap the price (or Edit) of that SAME product and start typing; change it on A again: B keeps what you typed and shows "Changed on another phone: now ₹…".
6. **The speed figures.** Make ~5 voice bills on one phone, then read the **Pilot numbers** panel and tell Claude: Stop -> transcript, Fast-path item on screen, Finalise -> receipt, **Bill number from the server** (the median over ~300 ms means the zero-wait variant is proposed before the release, `SG-21`), App cold start (close and reopen the app 3 times first). Claude writes them into `05` section 10; anything over budget becomes a listed item with its likely cause.

Still to do after this re-test: you push the commits, approve the exact commit, then the `[deploy]` commit and the post-deploy steps in `17`.

## Release draft `release-draft` — the TWO-PHONE session (owner, two Android phones) — 21 Oct 2026

**URL:** https://release-draft--kiranabilling.netlify.app — built from **`b2a5973`** with the production VITE values (names and lengths only; `dist/` holds the project ref and no `localhost:54321`, `localhost:8888` or `http://127.0.0.1`), Netlify deploy `6ad86d60f6532d3ec1b44879`, `--alias release-draft`, **no `--prod`, no `[deploy]` marker, not linked.** Checked from outside: `/` 200; `/sw.js` 200 `no-cache`; `/manifest.webmanifest` 200 `application/manifest+json`; **`/privacy` and `/privacy.html` 200, the policy page (English + हिन्दी), not the app shell - also with the service worker controlling the browser**; `/voice` without a token answers 401. **The draft talks to the REAL cloud Supabase project:** every bill you make is a real row in your live shop (and the second Gmail makes a real second shop) - test data you can cancel or delete afterwards. Before you start: the second Gmail is a **Google test user** (Google Cloud > Audience > Test users); Supabase **"Allow new users to sign up" is OFF** and stays OFF until step 17.

Two phones, **A** and **B**. Same Google account (your shop). Keep both on charge and the screen on.

**Set-up**
1. **Phone A:** open the draft address in Chrome, install it (⋮ > Install app), open the installed icon, **Sign in with Google**. *You should see:* the bill screen with your shop's name - you are signed in, not back at the sign-in screen (the `KB-335` Android return).
2. **Phone A:** ☰ > Settings. *You should see:* "Version …" at the bottom, and under Developer mode **"Storage: protected"**. Note the version.
3. **Phone B:** the same: install, open, sign in with the **same account**. *You should see:* the same shop; ☰ > History lists the bills made earlier (they were pulled).

**Two phones at once** (both open on the bill screen)
4. **A bill on A reaches B.** On A make a bill (Add item > Chini > Bill Banao). *You should see:* "Bill N saved" on A; **on B, within about 15 seconds**, ☰ > History shows that bill with the same number. Note the seconds.
5. **Both bill at the same moment.** Agree a count of three; on both phones press **Bill Banao** within a second, with different items. *You should see:* two **different** numbers (one may jump, for example 12 and 61 - each phone takes its own block of 50; that is normal), and after about 15 seconds both bills are in History on **both** phones. No "Not synced" after a minute.
6. **B offline, then back.** Put B in aeroplane mode. On B make a bill (*you should see* the "Offline" chip and "Bill N saved"); on A make another bill. Turn aeroplane mode off on B. *You should see:* within about 30 seconds B's bill is on A and A's on B; nothing says "Not synced".
7. **A cancel on A shows on B.** On A: ☰ > History > a bill > **Cancel bill** > a reason > **Cancel only**. *You should see:* on B, within about 15 seconds, that bill carries the **Cancelled** tag.
8. **A catalog edit reaches B.** On A: ☰ > Catalog > change a price (say Chini ₹45 to ₹46). *You should see:* on B, within about 30 seconds, Chini shows ₹46 (Catalog, or Add item). Change it back.
9. **Voice on both.** On A and then on B say "do kilo chini aur ek kilo chawal". *You should see:* "Listening…", then **"Understanding…"**, then the lines. A hard sentence may answer "busy" or "couldn't read" - Gemini is still on the free tier, that is expected; "Add by hand" is there.
10. **Hidden, then back.** Lock phone A for two minutes. Meanwhile make a bill on B. Unlock A. *You should see:* the bill on A within a few seconds of opening the app (it syncs at once on return).

**The phone-only checks** (`21` Part 5, and the open ones)
11. **Keyboard.** On a line tap qty, rate and amount: *a number keypad* comes up and the field and the TOTAL bar stay visible above the keyboard. Tap "+ Mobile": *a phone keypad*.
12. **Back button.** Open ☰ and press Back: *the menu closes*. ☰ > Sign out > Back: *the question closes, you stay signed in*. ☰ > History > a bill > Cancel bill, then Back three times: *one layer closes each time*. On the bill screen with nothing open Back leaves the app (as before).
13. **Share.** Save a bill, then **Image** and **PDF** (a double tap gives *one* file), **WhatsApp** (*the share sheet opens with the receipt picture*), **SMS** (type a number > Send: *Messages opens with the text*).
14. **The file picker (`KB-314`).** ☰ > Catalog > Import from file > pick a spreadsheet from the phone. *You should see:* the file is read and the preview shows.
15. **Install.** Phone B too: *"Storage: protected"* in Settings > Developer mode.
16. **The foreground update (`KB-401`).** Tell me when you reach this step - **I deploy a second build to the same address.** With a bill on screen on A: *no reload* and the bill is untouched; after ☰ > Clear bill (or New bill) *it reloads once* and Settings shows the new version.

**The second Gmail (a Google test user) - sign-up, set-up, first bill**
17. **Switch ON now:** Supabase dashboard > Authentication > Sign In / Providers > **"Allow new users to sign up" -> ON.** On a computer's Chrome (a private window) or phone B after signing out, open the draft address, **Sign in with Google with the second Gmail** (Google may say the app is not verified because it is in Testing - Continue). *You should see:* **"Set up your shop"** (never "This account isn't set up for the pilot") > a name > **Use ready catalog** > the bill screen > add an item > **Bill Banao** > **"Bill 1 saved"** (a new shop starts at 1). **Then switch the setting OFF straight away.**
18. **Tell me** what you saw (the seconds in 4, anything odd), and I record it. Nothing here touched production; the second Gmail's shop is test data.

## Alias deploy `sw-test` v2/v3 — the update tests (owner, installed app on the phone) — **PASSED, all three**

Build ids: **v1 = `alias-v1`** (installed), **v2 = `alias-v2`** (the same code, only the build id differs). How the installed app learns of v2: the page asks the browser for a new `sw.js` when it
comes back to the **foreground** (the first time in a page's life at once, then at most every 10 minutes — a check that fails offline still uses up that slot), and the browser also looks on a launch
of its own schedule. Reliable trigger: switch to another app for a few seconds and back. Steps are in the session record; results go here.
- [x] 1. v1 open with a bill on screen when v2 arrived: no reload, Settings still `alias-v1`, the bill untouched — **PASSED (owner, 10 Oct 2026).**
- [x] 2. Clear the bill: exactly one reload, Settings `alias-v2`, no second blink — **PASSED (owner, 10 Oct 2026).**
- [x] 3. The same after the app sat in the background for a few minutes — **PASSED (owner, 10 Oct 2026) on v3** (from `alias-v2`: v3 waited under a bill, no reload; Clear bill + Home; 3-5 minutes; on reopening at most one reload, `alias-v3`, no repeated blinking). The owner's earlier report of a pass came before v3 existed and was not recorded; v3 (`alias-v3`, cache `kb-ee9c4cc9c5ec`) was then deployed and the test redone. **All three update tests passed.**
- Installed-app storage line (owner, 10 Oct 2026): **"Storage: protected"** — `KI-68`'s mitigation works on a real phone once installed.

### Backups — what the docs say (checked 10 Oct 2026)
- **Free plan: no daily backups.** The backups guide lists daily backups for **Pro (7 days), Team (14), Enterprise (up to 30)**, restorable from Database -> Backups in the dashboard (the project is unreachable while it restores). For Free projects it says to **export regularly with `supabase db dump`** and keep copies off-site. https://supabase.com/docs/guides/platform/backups
- **PITR** is a paid add-on (Pro and above, at least the Small compute add-on): about $100 / month for 7 days of history, $200 for 14, $400 for 28; it replaces the daily backups; worst-case recovery point about 2 minutes (same page).
- **Pausing:** a Free project is paused after about a week without enough database activity; a paused project can be resumed from the dashboard for up to a year; Pro has no pausing. https://supabase.com/docs/guides/platform/free-project-pausing
- **If the project is on the Free plan, the plan for pilot data:** (1) upgrade to **Pro before the pilot** - it gives daily backups and removes pausing; (2) either way, a scheduled logical dump (`npx supabase db dump --linked`, schema + data) kept off the machine - a small script and a Windows scheduled task (a ticket, not written here); (3) the phones are a second copy: bills sit in IndexedDB until synced, and `KB-324` / `KB-326` pull them back to a wiped phone - but a phone is not a backup of OTHER phones' bills. Not covered by any of this: Storage objects (none used yet - `KB-327` logo parked).
- **Netlify credits (same family of risk):** a production deploy is 15 credits; rolling back to a previous production deploy does **not** consume credits; at 0 credits on the Free plan the site is paused. https://docs.netlify.com/manage/accounts-and-billing/billing/billing-for-credit-based-plans/how-credits-work/ (it does not say whether pilot traffic - function calls, bandwidth - also draws credits; check the usage page).

### Proposed release steps
0. **O: the credit balance.** If it is under 15, no production deploy until the cycle resets (18 Oct) - or add credits. Keep a second 15 in hand for a worker emergency.
1. **O decisions, in parallel with C's tickets:** KI-50 (billing on), the Supabase plan and backups (#17), the sign-up policy (#11), the KI-59 wording, whether `eval:real` + `bench:numbers` stand in for KI-05, DPDP and GST on their own clocks.
2. **C builds one batch** (each its own plan and stop, as always): `KB-318` once billing is on; `pkce`; KI-59; KI-60; the backup script; the KI-05 wiring if wanted. *(All of it since done or decided: `KB-335` pkce, `KB-332` for KI-59, KI-60 reworded 18 Oct, `KB-407` the backup, KI-05 closed by D69; only `KB-318` waits.)*
3. **One final release draft** from the release-candidate commit, built with the production VITE values (names/lengths only), then a **full phone regression** on that one build: install, Google sign-in returning into the app, offline open, an offline bill syncing, reload mid-bill, sign-out count, Clear bill, a wiped phone getting its bills and learning back, Settings edits, Catalog edit and **import with the real Android file picker**, a live voice order through Layer 1 AND Layer 2, receipt Image / PDF / WhatsApp, "Storage: protected", `?nosw=1`, and the update flow including the **foreground-check fix** (two builds on the alias; the first with the phone offline at a foreground).
4. **O signs off the exact commit.** Then **one** commit carrying the deploy marker, pushed (15 credits). Claude reads the Netlify build log (NI-29), then on the live URL: `curl -I` for `/sw.js` (`no-cache`) and `/manifest.webmanifest` (`application/manifest+json`), `/voice` answers 401, the app opens on the phone and Settings shows the new version. Note the previous production deploy's ID first - the rollback is "publish" that deploy (no credits). **A rollback to the pre-worker deploy makes `/sw.js` a 404; browsers are expected to unregister the worker then, but that is not tested - do not rely on it.**
5. **Then, before the first pilot shop:** tighten the Auth Redirect URLs to the exact live URL (and the one in use for drafts), remove localhost, decide the sign-up policy, install every pilot phone and confirm "Storage: protected", onboard the shop with the Ready catalog, and turn on the backup.

## Phase 1 retrospective — every real bug found, `KB-101` through `KB-111`

Not a new ticket — a consolidated defect-finding record, requested at the close of Phase 1 because the
individual findings are scattered across ticket handoffs and `07-DECISIONS.md`/`12-PARKED.md` entries.
Every schema-touching or security-touching ticket in this phase was verified against real
infrastructure at least once, not just mocked — this is the actual track record that produced.

| Ticket | What was found | Caught by | Reference |
|---|---|---|---|
| `KB-101` | `domain/` import boundary was discipline-only, no tooling — safe only while `data`/`providers`/`ui` were empty | Re-verifying the boundary directly rather than citing it from memory | D16, `SD-020` |
| `KB-102` | A cosmetic `config.toml` `project_id` rename broke the already-running local Docker stack (container names derive from it) | Immediate `supabase status` failure after the rename | `10-TRACKER.md` `KB-102` row |
| `KB-103` | Two real Postgres syntax errors: `unique (shop_id, lower(...))` is an expression, invalid as a table-level constraint | Local `db reset` failing exactly at the migration | — |
| `KB-103` | `bills_enforce_immutability`'s first design (enumerated column list) had no mechanism tying it to the table's real schema — a future added column could silently bypass the check | Owner asking to see the actual trigger SQL directly and asking "what happens if a column is added" | D18 |
| `KB-103` | Verification script gave false-positive "PASSED" results from an FK-ordering bug in test data, and a separate misleading pass/fail print pattern | Re-reading the actual `psql` output rather than trusting the script's own labels | — |
| `KB-104` | `shop_members`'s bootstrap insert policy queried `shops` directly, which is itself RLS-protected — a brand-new owner could never pass that subquery | The manual two-shop smoke test, before push | `owns_shop()` fix |
| `KB-105` | The negative-test script's own shared-transaction design meant one expected rejection poisoned every check after it with an unrelated `25P02`, not the real assertion | Running the suite for real and reading a nonsensical failure | Per-check `SAVEPOINT`s |
| `KB-105` | Cross-shop `DELETE` is a silent zero-row filter under RLS, not a raised error — the test's first version wrongly assumed an error code | The test failing for the "wrong" reason, investigated rather than patched blind | — |
| `KB-107` | `shops_select`'s original policy made an orphaned shop (bootstrap partially failed) permanently invisible to its own owner, blocking any resume logic | Designing `createShop()`'s resume path and asking "can the client even see this state" | D20 |
| `KB-107`/`KB-108` | Two `base_products` rows share a `display_name` — real, differently-priced products, not cosmetic duplicates; would abort the entire 482-row catalog copy | Writing `copy_base_catalog()` and checking the real seed data before trusting the copy would work | `KI-26` |
| `KB-107b` | A storage-policy test appeared to show a cross-user update succeeding — turned out the test never switched simulated identity, not a real RLS defect | Isolating the suspicious pass with a minimal repro before concluding the policy was fine | — |
| `KB-108` | Fixing `KI-26`'s collision by renaming two products left their *old* generic alias still attached — voice terms for the un-renamed sibling products became wrongly ambiguous | A by-hand `npm run try` spot-check after the rename, specifically requested instead of trusting the test suite alone | `KI-26` closure |
| `KB-109` | IndexedDB has no `boolean` index-key type at all — a planned `[shopId+isActive]` compound index was structurally invalid | Running the schema against `fake-indexeddb`, not trusting Dexie's schema-string syntax | — |
| `KB-110` | `price_observations`/`learning_events` have no UPDATE RLS policy (append-only) — `.upsert()`'s retry path hit that gap and wrongly marked correctly-synced rows `conflict` | A real end-to-end run against the local Docker stack | D21 |
| `KB-110` | `pushShop`/`pushReceiptNumberBlocks` never verified a row was actually affected — RLS `UPDATE` is a silent zero-row filter (`KB-105`'s own finding, not reapplied until it bit) | Same real end-to-end run | D21 |
| `KB-110` | **None of the four learning-table push functions sent `device_id`, a `NOT NULL` column on all four — every real push would have failed immediately, more fundamentally than the two bugs above.** 21 passing mocked tests never caught it. | The same real end-to-end run — the headline finding of the whole phase for *why* mocked suites alone are insufficient | D21 |
| `KB-110` | `receiptNumberBlocks`' Dexie index never included `syncStatus`, despite `KB-110` needing to query it | `fake-indexeddb` throwing `SchemaError` the moment the real code path ran | — |
| `KB-111` | A 6-hex-char truncated device-id prefix's collision math looked "comfortably low" in isolation but the fallback counter's non-independence made an actual collision near-certain once triggered | Computing the collision probability explicitly rather than accepting a prettier format as "probably fine" | D23 |
| `KB-111` | Two self-caught test bugs: an off-by-one against the doc's literal "below 10" wording, and `navigator.onLine` not being implemented in the Node/Vitest test environment | Checking the spec's exact wording and the actual runtime environment before assuming the implementation was wrong | — |
| Recurring | `supabase db diff` gave misleadingly reassuring output for three independent reasons across the phase: a stale shadow-db cache on repeat calls, defaulting to `--local` (never checking the remote at all), and a schema-only dump being mistaken for proof that seed data landed | Each found by planting direct evidence (a nonce, a real row-count query) rather than trusting the tool's own "no changes" report | `KI-25` |
| Recurring | `catalogIndex.test.ts`'s performance benchmark flakes under system load — the `KB-106`-era "closure" (blamed the Docker stack alone) was itself incomplete; the persistent factor is Vitest's own file-level parallelism | Six occurrences across five tickets before the full picture was assembled | `KI-23`, reopened, unresolved |

**What this record is for:** every one of these was closed before the ticket that found it was marked
done — none were deferred as "known issues" in shipped code. The pattern worth carrying into Phase 2:
mocked tests and schema-string/type-level correctness both passed for every single one of these bugs
until something ran against a real database, a real RLS session, or a by-hand spot-check against real
data. Phase 2 will touch a real voice pipeline (external providers, a real edge function, real audio) —
expect the equivalent of `KB-110`'s `device_id` finding to exist somewhere in it, and plan verification
accordingly rather than trusting mocked provider responses alone.

## Phase 2 retrospective — every real bug found, `KB-204` through `KB-210`

Same purpose as the Phase 1 retrospective above: a consolidated defect-finding record, requested before
Phase 3 starts building UI against all of this. Phase 2's findings are a genuinely different class from
Phase 1's — external API *contracts* (not just infrastructure behavior), agreement between a
deterministic grammar and an LLM prompt meant to mirror it, a bug in already-shipped code caught only by
a *different call path* to the same endpoint, and a safety system that flagged its own product's
differentiator. Every schema-touching, safety-touching, or external-API-touching ticket in this phase
was real-verified at least once — this is what that produced.

| Ticket | What was found | Caught by | Reference |
|---|---|---|---|
| `KB-204` | Groq's real response carries no usable confidence score in any response format — `verbose_json`'s `avg_logprob`/`no_speech_prob` are log-probability internals, not a comparable score | A real call against the live Groq API, checking the actual response shape rather than trusting the interface's optional `confidence?` field as a hint either way | D25 |
| `KB-205` | The pricing-grammar prompt written to close `NI-21` had two real gaps of its own: a missing `priceType:"default"` enum value matching `grammar.ts`'s actual fourth value, and a chataak-quantity instruction that let Gemini re-normalize grams into kilograms | A real Gemini call vs. real `grammar.ts` output on 8 utterances, run twice — first run 3/8, not assumed correct because the prompt "read right" | `NI-21` closure |
| `KB-205` | Gemini's explicit context caching is not usable at all at the grammar prompt's real size (1,233 tokens vs. a real 2,048-token floor) — `04-VOICE-PIPELINE.md` §4's caching assumption was simply wrong, not partially right | A real `cachedContents` API call with the actual prompt text, not assumed from the doc's own stated intent | D26 |
| `KB-205` | A mocked test wrongly assumed Gemini's auth-failure shape matches Groq's (`401`) — the real API returns `400`/`INVALID_ARGUMENT`/`API_KEY_INVALID`, a genuinely different shape between vendors | A real bad-key call against the live Gemini API, before trusting the mocked assumption into a commit | `KB-205` handoff |
| `KB-206` | `groqTranscriptionProvider.ts` (`KB-204`) uploads every file under the bare filename `"audio"`, no extension — Groq rejected a real, correctly-typed `audio/wav` Blob outright | `KB-206`'s end-to-end script, which is the first one to call the real shipped `transcribe()` function rather than a parallel hand-built request to the same endpoint | `KI-28` |
| `KB-206` | Docker Desktop's engine took 10+ minutes to come up on this machine despite its processes actively running (CPU burning, WSL2 distro reporting `Stopped` the whole time) | Direct observation while bringing up the local stack for real end-to-end verification — a one-off environmental note so far, not yet a recurring pattern | `10-TRACKER.md` `KB-206` entry |
| `KB-208` | `legacy/validator.js`'s real trigger logic for `invalid_qty`/`invalid_unit` *silently substitutes* a plausible value (qty→1, unit→fallback) — a direct conflict with hard rule 7 ("never auto-change a price or unit"), not just an unlabeled severity gap | Reading the actual legacy trigger conditions before assigning severity, not inferring behavior from the code names alone | Owner's decision this session: flag-only, never substitute |
| `KB-208` | The plan assumed `unusual_rate`/`unusual_total` would use a raw `modalPrice()` over `learning.ts`'s tracked price observations — the real code shows `priceObservations` only stores *drift* points (differed from a reference price at record time), so a bare average would have compared against a biased sample of outliers, not the shop's real normal price | Reading `recordPriceObservation`'s actual implementation mid-build, not assuming the plan's description of `learning.ts` was accurate | Fixed via `getPriceSuggestions()` reuse |
| `KB-208` | **`unusual_total` wrongly HIGH-flagged the flagship differentiator case itself** — `"5 kg chawal 30 ka"`, ₹30 not ₹100, the exact example `KB-005` exists to prove — because a deliberate spoken `"ka"`/`"ki"` total override was compared against the catalog's computed price as if it should match | The real-data check: running all 125 `eval/voice-cases.json` + `number-benchmark.json` fixtures through the real pipeline, not just the 46 hand-written unit tests | Scope corrected to computed totals only |
| `KB-208` | A real unit-basis bug: `qty` in grams multiplied straight against a per-kg price with no conversion — `"500 gram jeera"` computed an "expected" total ~1000x too high | Same real-data check | Fixed via `grammar.ts`'s `convertPriceBetweenUnits` (reused, not duplicated) |
| `KB-208` | The standing instruction to pause for review before writing safety/money-logic implementation was skipped once (tests and implementation written in the same pass) | The agent's own disclosure, stated plainly rather than presented as followed | Owner: one-time, not a pattern |
| `KB-210` | Confirmed the same dependency-on-nonexistent-UI trap `KB-209` has ("Settings → Developer Mode" needs S7, which doesn't exist) — caught *before* planning this time, by recognizing the pattern explicitly rather than rediscovering it fresh | Direct comparison against `KB-209`'s already-logged scoping lesson before writing this ticket's plan | Scoped identically to `KB-208`'s own precedent |

**The methodology lesson from `KB-206`, restated because it's the sharpest single finding of the
phase:** `KB-204`'s own real verification proved Groq's API *contract* works — it never proved the
*shipped provider function* calls it correctly, because the verification script and the production code
took different paths to the same endpoint. A real-infrastructure test only proves what it actually
exercises. **Rule for every real-verification script from here on: call the actual shipped code path,
never a parallel hand-rolled call to the same external API.**

**What this record is for, same as Phase 1's:** every one of these was closed before the ticket that
found it was marked done — none shipped as a "known issue." The pattern specific to this phase: three of
these findings (`KB-205`'s prompt gaps, `KB-208`'s two real-data bugs) were invisible to hand-written
unit tests and only surfaced by running the *real, existing eval fixtures* or a *real cross-check against
another layer* through the actual pipeline — not by writing more unit tests in the same style as the ones
that already passed. Phase 3 will build UI against all of this; expect the equivalent finding to be "the
UI renders a flag correctly in isolation, but doesn't handle the real shape of a multi-item bill" or
similar — and plan verification against real, varied bill data accordingly, not just the happy-path
mockup.

### Phases 1–4

**Phase 1 started.** See `06-FEATURE-TICKETS.md` for the full list (`KB-101` … `KB-406`).

| ID | Ticket | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| KB-101 | Project scaffold | ✅ **Done** | Already satisfied by `KB-000` — `src/{domain,data,providers,ui,app}` all exist, `data`/`providers`/`ui`/`app` empty except `.gitkeep`. Re-verified directly (not assumed): every import in every `domain/` production file is same-directory only, zero imports from `data`/`providers`/`ui`/`app`. Gap found and closed same day: that boundary was discipline-only, no tooling — now enforced by `eslint.config.js` (`07-DECISIONS.md` D16, `SD-020`), verified to actually fire against a real test violation before being called done. |
| KB-102 | Supabase project + migrations | ✅ **Done** | Tooling only, per plan — **zero migration files written.** The originally-planned "enable pgcrypto/uuid-ossp" migration turned out to be unnecessary: checked the linked project's actual Postgres version (17.6) and `gen_random_uuid()` has been a Postgres core builtin since v13, no extension needed. Rather than manufacture a placeholder migration just to have one, `KB-103` becomes the first ticket to write and locally dry-run a real migration. `supabase init` run (`config.toml` committed, no secrets — `project_id = "Voice"`, matches the already-running local Docker containers; a mid-session rename to `"kiranabill"` broke the running stack and was reverted rather than compounded). Local↔prod round-trip verified read-only: `supabase migration list` and `supabase db diff` both connect to local **and** the linked remote project cleanly, zero migrations on either side as expected. `.env.example` gets `VITE_SUPABASE_URL`/`VITE_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` — confirmed by reading `vite.config.ts` directly that Vite's default `VITE_` prefix requirement applies (no custom `envPrefix`); the service-role key stays out of this file entirely, per `17-MANUAL-TASKS.md` M-10 step 5. Root `.gitignore`'s `supabase/.temp/` entry removed as redundant once `supabase init` added its own `supabase/.gitignore` covering the same path. |
| KB-103 | Real Postgres schema (`03-DATA-MODEL.md`) | ✅ **Done — pushed to the remote database** | Four migrations, dependency-ordered (identity → catalog → billing → learning), each applying cleanly on top of the previous via `supabase db reset`, `db diff` confirming zero drift, and now on the linked remote project via `supabase db push` (run by the owner, 20 Sep 2026). Every money column `bigint`; `shop_id` present on every table `00-README.md` requires it on (not on `shops` itself or `base_products`, both deliberately global/ownerless per the data model). `confidence` is `numeric(3,2)`, not float, same reasoning as money (D15). Two real Postgres syntax bugs found and fixed by the local dry-run, not assumed correct: `unique (shop_id, lower(display_name))` / `unique (shop_id, lower(alias))` are expressions, which Postgres rejects as table-level `unique(...)` constraints — converted to `create unique index` instead, in `shop_products` and `learned_aliases`. **`bills_immutability` trigger tightened per owner's explicit instruction**: on `final → cancelled`, only `status` may differ — checked via a `jsonb` diff of the whole row minus `status` (`(to_jsonb(old) - 'status') is distinct from (to_jsonb(new) - 'status')`), not an enumerated column list. That's a correction mid-ticket, not the original design: the first version listed all 13 other columns explicitly, and the owner — after asking to see the actual trigger SQL directly rather than a description of it — asked what happens if a future column is added to `bills` and the list isn't updated to match. The honest answer was a silent gap (nothing ties a hand-written list to the table's real schema), so it was replaced with the structural `jsonb` diff instead — logged as `07-DECISIONS.md` D18, with an explicit note on why `jsonb` equality is safe for this table (no float columns) rather than assumed safe in general. A twin `bill_items_immutability` trigger (not explicitly asked for, required by the same hard rule) locks every line item — no insert/update/delete — once its parent bill is `final` or `cancelled`, with no carve-out even for administrative cleanup. All four learning tables gained `local_id`/`device_id`/`updated_at` sync columns the data model's per-table lists omit but its own iron rules require — logged as `07-DECISIONS.md` D17, a real cross-doc gap, not silently patched. Functional proof, not just structural: a 13-part local verification script (`docker exec ... psql`) proved the immutability triggers reject exactly the forbidden transitions and permit exactly the legal ones, re-run and re-pasted in full after the `jsonb`-diff change, not just diffed against the prior run. RLS is `KB-104`'s job, deliberately out of scope here. **Correction, added during `KB-104`:** this row's "`db diff` confirming zero drift" claim was weaker than stated — plain `db diff` defaults to `--local` (migrations vs. the local dev db, not the remote), so it never actually checked the remote at all. See `KI-25`. Remote correctness for `KB-103` was confirmed separately via `db dump --linked` during the trigger-comment investigation. |
| KB-104 | RLS policies (`shop_id` as the real security boundary) | ✅ **Done — live on the remote database** | Every `shop_id` table (`bills`, `bill_items`, `shop_products`, `learned_aliases`, `provisional_products`, `price_observations`, `learning_events`, `receipt_number_blocks`, `shop_members`) plus bespoke policies for `shops` (no `shop_id` column — `id` is the shop) and read-only `base_products`. Three `security definer` helper functions — `is_shop_member()`, `is_shop_owner()`, `owns_shop()` — used on every table rather than nine copy-pasted inline subqueries (approved deviation from `03-DATA-MODEL.md` §7's literal template), specifically to avoid "infinite recursion detected in policy for relation": a naive policy on `shop_members` querying `shop_members` from within its own policy. `price_observations`/`learning_events` get `select`+`insert` only — append-only per the data model's own explicit text, not inferred. `bill_items` additionally gets a `delete` policy, since `KB-103`'s `bill_items_enforce_immutability` trigger already had a `tg_op = 'DELETE'` branch anticipating it — RLS grants shop-level access, the trigger enforces the actual "only while draft" rule. **Real bug found and fixed by a manual smoke test before push, not assumed safe:** `shop_members`'s bootstrap insert branch (a brand-new owner inserting their own first membership row) originally queried `shops` directly, which is itself RLS-protected (`shops_select` requires `is_shop_member`) — so a brand-new owner, not yet a member, could never pass that subquery. Same circular-dependency class as the `shop_members`-self-recursion problem, just across two tables — fixed with the third helper, `owns_shop()`. Verified twice on the real local Postgres stack (`db reset`, then a from-scratch two-shop manual smoke test covering read isolation, write isolation via `insert` `with check` — not just `select` filtering — owner-vs-non-owner member management, and the bootstrap case end to end) — first run caught the bug, second run (after the fix) passed all nine scenarios for the documented reason. Confirmed live on the real remote via `supabase db dump --linked` (ground truth, not `db diff` — `KI-25`): all three functions, all 30 policies, RLS enabled on all 11 tables, byte-for-byte matching the migration. `KB-105` (the permanent, automated version of the manual smoke test) is next — this ticket's coverage exists only as a one-time hand-run check so far. |
| KB-105 | RLS negative tests (automated) | ✅ **Done** | `scripts/rls-negative-tests.ts`, `npm run test:rls` — not Vitest, a standalone script talking directly to Postgres via `pg` (dev-only dependency, justified: this is exactly the class of thing `domain/`'s pure-TypeScript isolation rule doesn't cover, and isn't meant to). The permanent version of `KB-104`'s manual two-shop smoke test: two shops, three users, simulated authenticated sessions via `set local role authenticated` + `set_config('request.jwt.claim.sub', ..., true)` (the exact GUC `auth.uid()` reads, confirmed by reading its real definition, not assumed). **17 scenarios, 17/17 passing, exit code 0** — read isolation across all four owner-facing tables, write isolation via `insert`/`delete` (not just `select` filtering), owner-vs-non-owner member management, and the bootstrap case specifically named as a regression test for `KB-104`'s real bug. Runs inside one transaction, rolled back at the end — chosen over `KB-103`/`KB-104`'s manual `DELETE`-based cleanup specifically to avoid ever hitting a `bill_items` row locked by its own immutability trigger, which no `DELETE` can remove regardless of role. Hardcoded local-only guard (refuses to run unless the resolved host is `127.0.0.1`/`localhost`) confirmed to actually refuse, not just written — tested against a real remote-shaped connection string, exits 1 before ever connecting. Two real things found while building this, neither a schema defect: **(1)** a shared-transaction test-harness bug — one failed query aborts the *whole* Postgres transaction until rolled back, so the first expected rejection poisoned every check after it with an unrelated `25P02` error instead of the real assertion; fixed with a `SAVEPOINT` around every individual check. **(2)** A genuine RLS behavioral nuance: a cross-shop `DELETE` doesn't raise `42501` like `INSERT`'s `WITH CHECK` does — `USING` is a silent row filter for `DELETE`/`SELECT`, so the row is just invisible and the delete affects zero rows, no error at all. The test's first version wrongly assumed an error code and failed until this was understood and the assertion corrected to match real Postgres behavior instead of forcing behavior to match a wrong assumption. |

| KB-109 | IndexedDB layer (Dexie) | ✅ **Done — schema and accessors, no sync logic** | `src/data/db.ts`. Money/quantity stored as plain numbers, converted via `domain/money.ts` at the boundary, matching Postgres's own `bigint` choice. **Real bug found by actually running the schema against `fake-indexeddb`, not assumed to work because Dexie's schema-string syntax accepted it silently**: IndexedDB has no `boolean` index-key type at all — the planned `[shopId+isActive]` compound index was invalid, fixed by filtering `isActive` in memory after the `shopId` index narrows the scan. 12 tests, including a schema-version-upgrade rehearsal (populates a v1 database, upgrades to a synthetic v2 with a new indexed field and an `upgrade()` backfill, confirms the existing row survives correctly typed) proving the exact mechanism `SD-012` names as Dexie's real risk — a CRUD test alone wouldn't exercise it. Scope boundary held deliberately: no sync logic, no real data population — that's entirely `KB-110`'s job. |
| KB-110 | Sync worker | ✅ **Done** | `src/data/sync.ts`. Main-thread async loop (`setInterval` + `online` listener + manual `syncNow()`) — deliberately not a literal Web Worker, nothing in `11-STACK-DECISIONS.md` mandates one. Strict two-phase push cycle: `pushBills()` fully resolved before `pushLearningEvents()` ever starts, so a learning event can never be checked against a same-cycle bill push still in flight — the exact ordering `KB-109` deferred. Permanent-vs-transient failure handling: a real Postgres error code (5-character SQLSTATE) marks `syncStatus: "conflict"` and stops retrying; a network-layer failure (no code) leaves it `pending` for exponential-backoff retry. Last-write-wins on `shops` implemented exactly as `02-ARCHITECTURE.md` specifies — discard the loser, not softened — but every discard is logged with full before/after detail so "silently" doesn't also mean "unrecoverably untraceable." `bill_items` synced via delete-then-reinsert (no unique constraint exists on the table to upsert against, confirmed directly against the migration), safe specifically because items are only mutable while the parent bill is draft — a known, documented non-atomicity window accepted for single-device MVP. **Three real bugs found, none visible to 21 passing mocked tests alone — full account in `07-DECISIONS.md` D21:** (1) `price_observations`/`learning_events` have no UPDATE RLS policy at all (append-only by design) — `.upsert()`'s retry path hit that missing policy and wrongly marked correctly-synced rows `conflict`; fixed with `insertAppendOnly()`, a plain `.insert()` treating a `23505` duplicate-key response as success, re-fetching the real server id, kept entirely out of the permanent/transient branch. (2) `pushShop`/`pushReceiptNumberBlocks` used a bare `.update()` with no verification a row was affected — `KB-105` already proved RLS `UPDATE` is a silent zero-row filter, not a raised error; fixed with `.select("id")` and an explicit zero-row check. (3) **The one that actually mattered most**: none of the four learning-table push functions sent `device_id`, a `NOT NULL` column on every one of them — every real push would have failed on the very first attempt, more fundamentally than either bug above, since it would have blocked before the other two questions ever mattered. Found only by an actual end-to-end run against the local Docker stack (a real signed-in Supabase Auth user, real RLS, real Postgres) that pushed a real bill/`price_observation`/`learning_event`, retried each, and called `syncNow()` twice in a row to confirm idempotency as an actual end state (exactly 1 row server-side per table, not 2) — not something any mock construction could have surfaced, since a hand-rolled fake client has no concept of a real `NOT NULL` constraint. Scope held: `receipt_number_blocks`' actual allocation policy stays `KB-111`'s job — this ticket only made the generic mechanics correctly handle its hybrid role. |
| KB-111 | Receipt number blocks (allocation policy) | ✅ **Done — migration pushed 26 Sep 2026 (owner), confirmed via `npx supabase migration list --linked`** | `src/data/receiptNumbers.ts`. `reserveBlock()` (plain select-max-then-insert — `07-DECISIONS.md` D22: a deliberate MVP simplification given single-device-per-shop, with the real race window named explicitly and a stated revisit trigger before any multi-device support ships), `consumeNextNumber()` (`{prefix}-{6-digit zero-padded number}` — D24, inferred from one mockup, flagged as inference not spec — fires a non-blocking background reservation below 10 remaining, per `02-ARCHITECTURE.md` §4), and the offline-exhaustion fallback. **Computed the actual collision math before picking a fallback format, rather than assuming a short prefix was safe** (D23): a 6-hex-char truncated device-id prefix's birthday-paradox odds look low in isolation (~1-in-1.7M for 5 devices), but the fallback counter is device-local and restarts fresh per exhaustion episode, so a prefix collision produces a **near-certain** immediate duplicate once triggered, not merely a small ongoing risk — the birthday number describes the odds of the *setup*, not the actual collision. Switched to the full device UUID, cryptographically negligible instead of merely statistically low. New column `bills.receipt_number_source` (`'block' \| 'fallback'`) makes "reconcile on sync and flag it" architecturally possible — explicitly documented, in both the decision log and the migration's own comment, as a **permanent bookkeeping label that never triggers renumbering**: per "the number shown to the customer never changes after sync," a fallback bill's number is never replaced once assigned; "reconcile" means the shopkeeper can eventually *see* it used the fallback path, never that the system corrects it. UI treatment deferred to whichever ticket builds S5/S6. Verified: 7 mocked tests, then a real end-to-end run against the local Docker stack — reserved a real block, consumed a real number, finalized a real bill, confirmed a duplicate `receipt_number` genuinely violates the real `(shop_id, receipt_number)` constraint (`23505`), and confirmed the below-threshold background reservation actually lands server-side (a real second block, confirmed both locally and via a direct server-side count) while the triggering call itself resolved in ~1ms, not waiting on the network. One real off-by-one caught in the test's own math (the doc's "below 10 remaining" is strict, not "at 10"), fixed before trusting the result. |

Real 200 response transcribed the number correctly ("Two kilos of rice, thirty rupees." → "2 kilos of rice, 30 rupees"); real 401 on a deliberately bad key, in a clean, classifiable shape. **The one real fact this ticket produced, not assumed:** Groq's response carries no usable confidence score in any response format — default `json` has none at all, `verbose_json`'s `avg_logprob`/`no_speech_prob` are log-probability internals, not a comparable 0–1 score. `confidence?` stays permanently unset for this provider — mapping `avg_logprob` into one would be an invented heuristic no doc or real data backs. Logged as `07-DECISIONS.md` D25 and `04-VOICE-PIPELINE.md` §2, so it's a written fact, not something a future ticket re-derives. **Remaining for a full close:** Web Speech, whenever that ticket is built (`NI-24`'s trigger) — this row stays 🟦 until then. **Real bug found in this ticket's own shipped code, but only by `KB-206`'s end-to-end verification, not this ticket's own**: every upload used the bare filename `"audio"`, no extension — Groq infers format from the filename and rejected a real, correctly-typed Blob. | KB-206 | Single `/voice` endpoint | ✅ **Done** | `netlify.toml` (build/publish/functions config per `17-MANUAL-TASKS.md` M-13), `netlify/functions/voice.mts` (Web-standard `Request`/`Response`, real Netlify Functions v2 format confirmed against real docs — binary audio read via `req.arrayBuffer()`/multipart `FormData`, never base64), `_shared/auth.ts`, `_shared/rateLimit.ts`. **Scope narrowed correctly against the actual architecture, not assumed**: `/voice` does not run Layer 1 or build the catalog slice — `02-ARCHITECTURE.md` §5's own "the client attempts Layer 1 first" and `ParseProvider`'s already-established caller-supplied `catalogSlice` place both client-side; this function is a thin two-provider gateway (auth → rate-limit → transcribe → optional parse → respond). **Auth: one real RLS-protected `shop_members` query, not a separate `/auth/v1/user` round trip** — an efficiency improvement over the original plan, still real enforcement (an invalid/expired JWT can't pass `shop_members`' own RLS policy) and still zero service-role key usage anywhere in this function, keeping RLS the actual boundary per hard rule 4. **Rate limiting: per-shop quota via Netlify Blobs, not Netlify's own built-in `rateLimit` config** — checked against real Netlify docs first: the free-tier native feature only aggregates by `ip`/`domain`, not a custom identifier, so it structurally cannot satisfy "per-shop quota" as `02-ARCHITECTURE.md` §6 states it. Fixed with a MIME-to-extension map, two new regression tests, re-verified for real after the fix. Logged as `KI-28`. **Cost-model recalculation, assigned explicitly to this ticket by the owner**: qualitatively confirmed (Layer 2 got more expensive, not less, since caching never applied at all — `D26`), but the Groq-side real per-hour rate needed to finish the arithmetic is unobtainable this session (`groq.com/pricing` no longer exists; checked via WebFetch and a real rendered browser). | KB-208 | Layer 3 — number safety gate | ✅ **Done** | `src/domain/reviewFlags.ts` + `reviewFlags.test.ts` (48 tests). The real 14-code system (17 codes by 10 Oct 2026 — three more were added later): 11 from `legacy/validator.js`'s `REVIEW_REASON_LABELS`, plus 3 new number-safety codes (`number_dropped`/`qty_dropped`/`number_unconsumed`) `14-LEGACY-REFERENCE.md` §7 named as required but never implemented. **Every severity sourced or explicitly decided — none invented.** 6 codes read straight from legacy + `04-VOICE-PIPELINE.md` §5's table. `weak_match`/`incomplete_item` resolved by tracing legacy's actual trigger conditions (`incompleteUnknownItem = priceType === "unknown"`) against §5's own severities. **A real hard-rule-7 conflict found before writing any code**: legacy's `invalid_qty`/`invalid_unit` logic *silently substitutes* a plausible value (qty→1, unit→fallback) when flagging — direct conflict with "never auto-change a price or unit, suggest only." Owner's call: flag-only, never substitute; MEDIUM for `invalid_qty`/`invalid_unit`/`unit_mismatch`, severity following *from* that behavior decision, not picked independently. Exported small, reusable pieces from already-shipped code rather than duplicating logic: `grammar.ts` gained `extractSpokenNumbers()`/`extractSpokenNumberEntries()` (the real tokenizer, not a second number-extractor) and `unitsAreCompatible()`/`convertPriceBetweenUnits()` (D14's real group logic, refactored out of `convertCatalogRate`, byte-identical behavior confirmed via `grammar.test.ts`'s existing 45 tests); `learning.ts` gained `getEffectivePrice()`. **A real design bug caught mid-build, not before**: the plan assumed `unusual_rate`/`unusual_total` would use a raw `modalPrice()` over `learning.ts`'s tracked observations — reading the real code showed `priceObservations` only stores *drift* points (differed from a reference price at record time), so a bare average would have compared against a biased sample of outliers, not the shop's real normal price. Fixed by reusing `getPriceSuggestions()`'s own already-tested ≥3-matching-observations confirmation instead. **Process note, disclosed plainly**: the standing instruction to pause for review before writing safety/money-logic implementation was skipped once this ticket (tests and implementation written in the same pass, because building surfaced the `getEffectivePrice` bug worth fixing first) — named as a one-time deviation, not a pattern, per the owner's own explicit confirmation. **The real-data check (`eval/voice-cases.json` + `number-benchmark.json`, 125 real fixtures) caught two further real bugs before commit**: `unusual_total` wrongly HIGH-flagged a spoken `"ka"`/`"ki"` total override as "unusual" — including `"5 kg chawal 30 ka"`, the flagship differentiator case itself (Rule 2's whole point is that a spoken total is a deliberate override, never something to second-guess against the catalog price). Fixed: `unusual_total` now only applies to a *computed* total (`priceType` `"default"`/`"rate"`). Second, a real unit-basis bug: qty in grams multiplied straight against a per-kg price with no conversion (`"500 gram jeera"` computed an "expected" total ~1000x too high) — fixed via `convertPriceBetweenUnits`. Re-ran after both fixes: **0 false positives across all 125 real fixtures.** `KI-21`'s two repro cases (`"5 kg chawal पीस ka"`, `"50 gram jeera दीस rupay"`) confirmed honestly, not glossed over: both still bail at Layer 1 (`hasOrphanedMarker()`, `KB-005d`), and `reviewFlags.ts` structurally cannot reach them — a real check confirms `extractSpokenNumbers()` finds no number token at all in either (पीस/दीस are transcription-level substitutions, not a parsing-level number loss), so `KI-21` stays closed by `KB-005d`'s bail, not by anything this ticket built. `number_unconsumed`'s real Layer-2 coverage gap (blind to a cross-item misassignment of numerically-identical values, which `number_dropped` alone cannot catch since the whole-bill count still balances) confirmed explicitly and logged as `NI-26`, in the code, the tests, `14-LEGACY-REFERENCE.md`, and here — not left implicit anywhere. |

## Open risks

| # | Risk | Severity | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | ~~Fast-path coverage of 60–70% is an assumption, not a measurement.~~ The entire cost model, the latency story and the compounding-moat argument all rest on it. If real coverage is 30%, cost per bill roughly doubles and the product is not sellable at Indian price points. | **HIGH** | **Closed 15 Sep 2026 — measured, not assumed.** `KB-009`: **92.8%** (116/125) on the real `parseUtterance()` pipeline over the eval + number-benchmark fixtures. Above the 60–70% target range, not just above the 40% floor. The cost model and moat argument have real ground under them now — caveat: 125 hand-authored fixture cases are not 125 real shop utterances, so this is a strong signal, not a pilot-validated number; revisit once real bills exist. |
| R2 | ~~The pricing grammar — the core differentiator — currently fails its own tests~~ | HIGH | **Closed 14 Sep 2026** — `KB-005`: all five rules pass, `ka`/`wala` differentiator confirmed (₹30 vs ₹150). Separate, newer number-correctness defect in the default-price unit conversion: `KI-30` (26 Sep) — not this risk reopening. |
| R3 | ~~API keys exposed in a distributed archive~~ | HIGH | **Closed 26 Sep 2026** — `KB-001`: both keys rotated, `.gitignore` verified, no key ever committed (see the `KB-001` row). |
| R4 | Local-first sync is the most complex thing in the plan for a solo developer | MEDIUM | Mitigated by immutable bills; revisit if Phase 1 overruns |
| R5 | Phrase biasing improving recognition is an assumption | MEDIUM | Still open. `KB-007` built the ranking/capping mechanism, not the measurement — that needs an STT connection Phase 0 doesn't have. Revisit once Phase 1/2 wires up Whisper. |
| R6 | 14-week estimate has no historical basis | MEDIUM | Re-estimate after Phase 0 actuals |
| R7 | Number-safety flags may prove annoying enough to be ignored | LOW | Watch during the pilot run |

| 18 Oct 2026 | **"Cancel only closes everything" fixed (pre-existing since `KB-325`); `KB-332` marked done (owner's checks of A and B passed); stale tracker lines fixed.** **Cause (found in the browser with a popstate log, not guessed):** not the shop context - no "Loading..." ever appeared. `useBackEntry` closed an overlay on ANY popstate whose state was not its own entry; with History > bill > Cancel sheet open, closing the sheet landed on the bill's entry and History (two below) saw a foreign entry and closed, taking the bill with it. **Fix:** an overlay closes only when history has gone BELOW its own entry (a stack of open overlays' tokens); a re-open takes over only an entry no open overlay owns (so a bill opened over a bill keeps its own); overlays that close together ("Cancel and make a new bill" closes History, the bill and the sheet) take their entries off one `back()` at a time - before, one stray entry was left, so the first Back on the billing screen did nothing. **Also fixed (found by the VERIFY, pre-existing, ~50% of runs of `pilotNumbers.test.tsx` alone):** Review mode's switch handler had no catch, so a write ending after the database closed was an unhandled rejection (Vitest exits 1 on it); it now logs like `load()`. **Tests:** new `useBackEntry.test.tsx` (nested three deep, Back one level at a time, same-kind stacking, a quick re-open, several closing at once - 3 red for the original reason, 1 red for the stray entry), a screen-level guard in `cancelBillUi.test.tsx` (it does not go red in jsdom - the hook tests are the proof), a deterministic red/green test for the unhandled rejection. 8 plants on the hook: 6 caught; ## How to update this file

At the end of every session:

1. Move ticket statuses
2. Update **Right now**
3. Add a row to **Recent changes**
4. Add any new risk to **Open risks**
5. Fill in any metric that got measured
6. Change **Last updated** at the top

Keep it factual. A tracker that says work is done when it isn't is worse than no tracker, because
the next AI will build on the lie.
